# Combat, actions and timing — provisional playtest integration

**Status: REVIEWED / reference-VALIDATED within the supplied combat/action/timing/vision scope.** [GPT's disposition](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_STRAIN_FIRST_REVIEW.md) confirms the corrected candidate. This does not close all combat, movement, encounter or condition work. Original books, class cards and earlier drafts are preserved. The NEW decisions below apply to this candidate only.

Read alongside [core resolution and injury](CORE_RESOLUTION_PLAYTEST.md), [conditions](CONDITIONS_PLAYTEST.md), [custom riders](CUSTOM_CONDITION_BINDINGS.md), [spell integration](SPELL_INTEGRATION_RULES.md) and [shared class rules](CLASS_PLAYTEST_RULES.md). This document explicitly supersedes conflicting provisional clauses only where listed. A specific reviewed feature retains its printed costs, prerequisites and exceptions.

## Source decisions

The [exact packet](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/COMBAT_ACTION_VISION_SOURCE_PACKET.json) contains 217 paragraphs from unchanged Core V2 and V6 books. Paragraph indices are zero-based all Word XML `w:p` descendants. [Source validation](COMBAT_SOURCE_VALIDATION.json) verifies quotations and whole-book hashes. The [first GPT proposal](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_COMBAT_ACTION_TIMING_FIRST_REVIEW.md), [counterexamples](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/CODEX_COMBAT_COUNTEREXAMPLES.md) and [revised decisions](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_COMBAT_COUNTEREXAMPLE_REVIEW.md) preserve the conference.

| Topic | Source / conflict | Candidate decision |
|---|---|---|
| Initiative, action list, encounter clock | Core 373–418 | Core controls; compatible V6 8066–8244 supplies detailed procedures. |
| Reaction | Core/V6 summarize one per round; V6 and class integration refresh own turn | NEW explicit interpretation: one resource between regular own-turn starts; no global-round reset. |
| Stances | Core 419–435; extra-turn ambiguity | Keep listed effects; NEW regular-turn selection and finite allowances. |
| Movement | Core 436–458; V6 mode switching subtracts physical distance | NEW spent-cost ledger prevents terrain/standing refunds; multiple legal Dashes each add Speed. |
| Maneuvers | Core 460–486 | Preserve sourced effects; NEW declaration, costs and endpoints fill missing execution details. |
| Grapple/Shove | V6 opposed procedure vs older shared-class generic DC | Ordinary maneuvers use V6 opposed rolls. Printed card saves/DCs retain their scope. |
| Prone and Invisible | Core/V6 conflicts already reviewed | Current condition reference controls, including Core ranged Prone disadvantage and Invisible position-reveal exception. |
| Round duration / simultaneous events | Sources do not give complete ordering | NEW preserved virtual markers and explicit start/end sequence below. |
| Ready | V6 casts and holds spell; generic Action or movement | Preserve that procedure; NEW one pending state and preparation/release details. |
| Hide/Search | Core lists actions; V6 unseen attacks | NEW passive DC and observer-specific Hidden procedure using Core task degrees. |
| Diagonals / terrain composition | 5-ft tile convention; missing full composition | NEW equal orthogonal/diagonal metric and additive movement costs. |
| Periodic and falling clocks | Existing reviewed overlays | Remain authoritative in their stated scope; no extra-turn tick or duplicated fall step. |
| Equipment, cargo and Sunder | Core delegates actual object/cargo systems | No invented durability, capacity or automatic cargo protection. These dependencies remain open. |

## Encounter and action resources

Establish terrain, light, hazards, objectives, exits and cargo; determine awareness; roll `d20 + DEX modifier + legal bonuses`. Highest first. Players choose player/player ties; the Director resolves ties involving NPCs. Keep the resulting ordered slots for the encounter. A round represents approximately six seconds. A **regular turn** is a creature's normal initiative slot even if it cannot act. An extra turn is distinct.

A turn normally permits movement, one Action, a Bonus Action when an actual rule grants a use, and one ordinary free object interaction. A second ordinary interaction or an Action-time object activation uses an Action. Brief communication is not an extra Action. Conditions and specific features may restrict these permissions.

The shared limit is one **extra full Action per creature per numbered round**. Such an Action cannot grant another extra Action, Bonus Action or Reaction. This cap is independent of Reaction timing. Extra turns do not by themselves refresh Reactions, recharge, periodic ticks, recovery ticks or the regular-turn allowances defined here.

**Reaction interpretation.** Begin an encounter with one available Reaction unless surprised or otherwise prohibited. Refresh at each regular own-turn start; never at the numbered round boundary or an extra turn. A late-initiative creature can therefore react before its first turn, refresh, and react again in the same numbered round. A legal trigger, available resource and all feature prerequisites are still required.

**Explicit additional Reactions.** A reviewed card can grant a separate limited use without refreshing the ordinary resource. Warrior Mind Shock supplies its stated once-per-duration non-attack Reaction; spending one resource does not consume/reset the other. Surprise, Incapacitated and Focused still restrict both. It grants no Counterspell knowledge or waived costs. Extra turns refresh neither. Per-dose reapplication/stacking and other specific grants remain part of the class semantic pass.

**Surprise — V6 plus NEW Bonus Action clarification.** On a surprised creature's first regular turn it cannot voluntarily move or take an Action or Bonus Action. It cannot react until that turn ends. Its slot, durations, passive effects, concentration, periodic effects and recharge still occur. Surprise alone is not Incapacitated or Unconscious. Brief communication and an otherwise legal simple free interaction remain possible.

**Entry — NEW.** Standard end-round reinforcements roll/receive initiative and first act next round. An explicit midround entrant acts this round only if its complete ordered slot is still upcoming; entry during that exact slot counts as already passed. It has its initial Reaction unless prohibited. A summon-specific timing rule overrides this generic entry procedure.

After the final slot, resolve Core's end-round hazards, reinforcements, morale, objectives and boss phases. Conclude the scene with surrender, capture, pursuit, harvesting access and noise consequences under their own rules; winning initiative does not resolve those automatically.

## Time and event ordering — NEW

For a duration created in initiative, record the relevant original regular start/end marker. Existing effects keep that **virtual marker**, repeating every six seconds if the creature leaves or changes initiative. Reordering, rerolling, extra turns, death/removal or leaving/reentering initiative never restart an existing duration. New effects use the current schedule. Source death still ends concentration, a source-dependent aura/tether or an effect explicitly ending on death.

- A bare hostile target-bound **N rounds** ends at the Nth future regular target-end marker. An in-progress target turn's current end does not count.
- A bare self/source buff, ally-targeted buff or source-controlled aura **N rounds** ends at the Nth future source-start marker. Explicit target-turn, pulse or absolute timing overrides this default. Activation during source turn 1 for three rounds ends at source turn 4's start.
- **Next turn** means the next future regular turn of the named creature, not the current turn's still-upcoming end. An unqualified next turn refers to source/user under the existing shared rule. Explicit “this turn” can include the current turn.
- An outside-initiative bare N-round effect instead gets an absolute application-time + N×6 seconds deadline. Entering initiative preserves that deadline.
- Explicit periodic profiles retain their registered clocks and tick counts. Never create a second timer merely because a generic duration rule also exists.

The [exact GPT review](../../../../docs/audit/2026-10-08/full-game/GPT_COMBAT_EXACT_FINDINGS.md) confirms ally-buff source clocks and excluding the current turn for explicit next-turn endpoints. The helper excludes a specific current-end marker only if that marker would otherwise be selected: application during a turn at time1 with end3 excludes3 and selects9; application at/after3 also selects9, without erroneously skipping again to15.

At a regular turn's **start**, resolve in this order:

1. Expire start-ending effects and apply immediate state changes from those expirations.
2. Refresh Reaction; tick regular-start recharge/non-action clocks.
3. Resolve already-due absolute state recovery if now legal, including a previously blocked Stable recovery deadline.
4. If still alive, dying and at 0 HP with death saves, make that turn's death save.
5. Resolve other start effects and periodic events in application order.
6. Begin voluntary turn decisions.

At **end**, resolve triggered effects, due damage, saves and maintenance before end-ending effects expire; then the slot finishes. No extra turn creates another regular marker. The caller must not reuse a resolved marker.

For simultaneous effects, earlier application timestamp precedes later. For simultaneous components of one source, its printed internal order controls; otherwise use stable application sequence, with source/mechanical ID as a final tie-break. Record that sequence when creating the effects. Beneficial/harmful labels do not change priority. Periodic family arbitration happens once per family/marker before its damage, retaining the family registry's consumed loser ticks.

Examples: dying at 0 rolls its death save before ordinary start-turn healing; a fatal death save is not undone by ordinary healing. Stable at 0 skips the save, then start-turn damage can break Stable. A natural 20 can restore 1 HP before a due Bleed drops it to 0 as a new drop event. If Decay expires while a Stable recovery deadline is already due, the old deadline resolves once without rerolling recovery time.

## Standard Actions

| Action | Procedure |
|---|---|
| Attack | Make the attacks legally granted by class and weapon. Replacing one attack does not replace the entire Action unless specified. |
| Cast | Use an Action-time spell or Resonance technique; all known/prepared, cost, component and target requirements apply. |
| Dash | Add one current Speed to each mode's turn cap; see movement ledger. Speed 0 grants none. A second legally paid Dash adds another Speed. |
| Disengage | Voluntary movement does not provoke opportunity attacks for the rest of that turn. |
| Dodge | Until next regular start, attacks from attackers you can see have disadvantage; your DEX saves have advantage. Benefit fails while Incapacitated or Speed 0. |
| Guard | +2 AC against the first attack against you before next regular start; consumed on that attack hit or miss. Guard state and Interpose permission remain until expiry even after the +2 is spent. |
| Aim | Action; no prior voluntary movement this turn, and no subsequent voluntary movement that turn. +2 to one ranged/spell attack before turn end. Forced movement does not cancel it. It grants no attack; a legal Bonus Action/extra Action/express attack must supply one. |
| Help | Advantage on a relevant ally check before next regular start, or assist an attack against a foe within 5 ft: the helped ally's first attack before that deadline has advantage. Assistance must be physically possible. |
| Hide / Search | Use the observer-specific procedures below. |
| Ready | Prepare an Action or movement; release uses Reaction as detailed below. |
| Use Object | Pay for the second ordinary interaction or an object's required Action activation. No extra hands/capacity are granted. |
| Improvise | Director declares ability, applicable proficiency, DC/opposed method and intended consequence before rolling. Success does not create unannounced conditions/actions. |

**Brace — NEW.** Action, choose one mode until next regular start. **Intercept:** choose a breach/doorway edge or current melee reach; when a hostile creature charges/leaps/breaches into it and enters legal reach, spend Reaction for one legal melee weapon attack after entry and before later movement/actions. **Hold Ground:** spend Reaction on the first applicable discrete push/pull/shove/knockback to reduce displacement 5 ft, minimum 0. It does not reduce damage, prevent independent Prone, stop gravity/fall steps, currents, conveyors, collapsing ground or teleportation, or grant support/hover. One Brace supplies one opportunity, not an extra Reaction.

**Rally — NEW.** Action; choose one ally within 30 ft able to perceive the rally and one active source-qualified Frightened record. CHA check at that record's source DC, otherwise 10; Success/Critical Success ends only that record. A later reviewed morale rule can supply a separate specific interaction. No automatic temporary HP, loyalty, belief or advantage.

**Withdraw.** Action; separate half-current-Speed movement budget, normal terrain costs, no opportunity attacks from that movement and +2 AC only while that movement resolves. Speed 0 grants none. Moving zero grants no lingering +2. Ordinary movement before/after remains separate. The separate budget is NEW interpretation of Core 480.

**Secure Cargo.** Core 482 preserves the wording “up to one Extraction Load class.” The subsequently reviewed [body and carrying guide](BODY_EQUIPMENT_PLAYTEST.md) supplies the provisional interpretation: spend an Action on one legal attach, lift, transfer or protective operation involving one actual cargo unit or physically constituted bundle with an assigned Extraction Class. Load measures burden; Extraction Class measures danger and logistics. Apply actual capacity, custody, hands, harnesses, checks and durability. The Action grants no extra capacity, equipment, automatic success, invulnerability or reduction in Extraction Class. Full equipment and extraction catalogs remain open.

## Ready

Spend an Action to declare a perceivable trigger and one legal Action-time action or movement. It expires next regular own-turn start. **NEW:** only one pending Ready per creature; replacing it loses the prior setup and any paid held-spell costs. No Bonus Action/Reaction ability, long ritual or multi-minute activity can be prepared by generic Ready.

The trigger happens first, then the readied response immediately follows before later events, unless a specific effect explicitly interrupts. On release spend the normal Reaction and recheck target, range, sight/perception, line of effect, ammunition and other prerequisites. An invalid or declined occurrence spends no release resource; the Ready can wait for another valid occurrence before expiry. This does not undo setup costs. Once released, it is consumed.

For ordinary nonspell actions, feature/ammunition costs are paid on release unless text charges setup. A readied **Attack Action** gets its legally available ordinary attack count; a feature limited to your own turn does not thereby work off-turn. Core Overwatch is explicitly one ranged attack, not that full Attack Action and not Soldier's distinct engine.

A readied spell is actually **cast at preparation**: choose spell/mode, verify legality, pay RP/material/other costs, provide components, resolve Dissonance/Refrain and cast-trigger effects once. Counterspell/cast-interruption opportunity occurs then, not again at release. Every held spell requires concentration, even ordinarily non-concentration spells; incompatible prior concentration ends. Lost concentration loses the held spell and costs. NEW: select the legal target/area point at release and recheck legality without repaying. At release the hold continues as ordinary spell concentration if required, otherwise ends after resolution. Expired/replaced/countered held spells do not refund paid costs.

Ready movement grants a separate current-Speed budget on release, pays terrain costs and can provoke ordinary opportunity attacks. It grants no extra Action, Bonus Action or interaction.

Focused stance's Reaction restriction also blocks an ordinary Ready release; Ready does not create a Counterspell exception. Holding a spell does not waive any current Reaction prohibition.

**Spell-effect duration begins at release.** Its normal duration is anchored when the effect manifests, while Ready expiry and held concentration already run from preparation. A source explicitly tying a particular consequence to casting/preparation retains that consequence's stated clock. No casting cost, Refrain, Dissonance or cast trigger occurs a second time merely from release.

## Stances

Choose one stance during a **regular** own turn before the first event that would use any stance benefit/restriction: attack, spell, relevant check/save or movement. If none occurs, choose before turn end. No retroactive selection. It lasts until next regular start. NEW: extra turns preserve the stance and spent allowances; they cannot choose/replenish it.

| Stance | Effect |
|---|---|
| Aggressive | +2 damage once per qualifying weapon attack's damage packet; −2 AC. Not periodic or copied damage. |
| Guarded | +1 AC, advantage on explicit roll/contest resisting Shove, Speed −5 (minimum 0). Its +1 can coexist with Guard's first-attack +2, giving +3 then +1. This combination is explicitly allowed here. |
| Mobile | First 5 ft of actual voluntary movement does not provoke; no free movement is granted. Your opportunity attacks reduce each base weapon die one step: d12→d10→d8→d6→d4 (minimum d4). Rider dice unchanged. Die sequence is NEW. |
| Focused | Choose +1 save DC for one spell OR +1 to one attack roll from one spell. After use, no-Reaction-except-Counterspell restriction remains through expiry. This grants no Counterspell knowledge, free cost or extra Reaction. |
| Carrying | Choose one actual currently applicable cargo penalty to ignore; choice fixed through expiry. It does not increase capacity or erase all load limits. DEX saves have disadvantage. |

## Maneuvers and ordinary attacks

All opposed checks use Core total comparison: higher wins, exact tie preserves state. No natural-20 automatic victory or hidden task-degree effect on an opposed tie. A printed save-based class maneuver keeps its own method.

| Maneuver | Execution |
|---|---|
| Grapple | Replace one Attack Action attack; reach and target ≤one size larger. STR Athletics vs defender STR Athletics/DEX Acrobatics. Escape Action uses same opposed contest. NEW one free usable grasping hand/appendage per target; release freely on own turn. Ends if required reach breaks or grappler becomes Incapacitated. No concentration required. Dragging halves Speed unless target ≥two sizes smaller. Magical/card exceptions remain source-specific. |
| Shove | Replace one attack; same reach/size/contest limits; success pushes 5 ft away or knocks Prone. |
| Trip | Replace one melee attack, Athletics vs Athletics/Acrobatics, success Prone. NEW same one-size-larger cap. |
| Disarm | Replace one attack, Athletics or weapon check vs Athletics/Acrobatics. Item must be held and reachable. Attacker chooses legal adjacent unoccupied drop space, otherwise target's own space. Explicit non-droppable bonds prevent it; magic/curse alone does not. No severed attunement/ownership/curse. |
| Feint | Bonus Action Deception vs Insight; success advantage on next melee attack this turn only. |
| Sunder | Attack an object/shield/armor component/construct joint using its actual durability system rather than creature HP. Unspecified durability remains an equipment dependency. |
| Break Formation | Special Shove replacing one attack, same reach/size contest with attacker disadvantage. Success pushes 10 ft; snapshot target's adjacent allies before push and remove their Guard Action benefits. Does not remove Guarded stance or target's own Guard. |
| Overwatch | Ready one ranged attack against movement in a named lane. Reaction and ammunition paid at release. Distinct from Soldier's class engine. |

**Pin and Silence the Caster — NEW tradeoff.** Declare before rolling and take **−2 to that attack roll**. Each creature has one Pin token and one Silence the Caster token between starts of its regular turns. An unused token may be spent on any otherwise legal qualifying attack, including off-turn, extra-turn or readied attacks. A legal declaration followed by an attack roll consumes the token even on a miss; an illegal declaration rejected before rolling consumes nothing. Tokens refresh only at regular own-turn start, never on an extra turn or numbered-round boundary. One attack cannot combine another optional tactical maneuver. These provisional numbers require table playtesting.

Pin uses a ranged weapon attack. On hit deal ordinary damage and apply Speed −10 (floor 0), DC `8 + PB + attack ability modifier`. Ordinary Pins do not stack the penalty. At target's regular start, one optional STR Athletics task check against the highest active ordinary Pin DC ends all ordinary Pins on Success/Critical Success. Alternatively one Action removes all automatically. Source-specific card Pins do not silently acquire this generic definition or removal scope.

Silence the Caster uses a melee attack. On hit prevent verbal-component Reactions until target's next regular start. If concentrating, make exactly one CON concentration save at `max(10, floor(post-mitigation damage/2))`, including DC10 when damage is zero. This fulfills, rather than duplicates, the normal damage concentration check. It is not Core Silenced: speech and Action-time verbal casting remain possible.

**Interpose.** While Guarding, after an adjacent ally becomes an attack target but before its roll, spend Reaction and become target if legal for that attack (range/type/line/cover). NEW may move **up to** 5 ft using a separate terrain-paying budget, including zero if already legally positioned. Speed0 permits the zero-movement case only. It redirects one attack without cancelling or duplicating it. Guard's unspent +2 applies if this is the first attack against you. Voluntary movement has ordinary opportunity-attack consequences.

**Press.** On a melee hit during your regular turn, arm one target until next regular start. While unconsumed, another qualifying hit during that turn may replace it. If the target voluntarily retreats from an adjacent space, follow up to 5 ft into the vacated space using a separate terrain-paying budget; no Action/BA/Reaction. It can provoke ordinary opportunity attacks. No following teleport/forced movement. Once used, cannot rearm before next regular turn, including during an extra turn. Speed0 prevents movement.

**Opportunity attack.** Spend Reaction for one melee attack against a seen hostile creature voluntarily leaving reach, immediately **before** exit. Forced movement/teleport do not trigger; Disengage suppresses it for its stated turn. Specific reviewed triggers may override these conditions.

**Two-weapon fighting.** After taking Attack and attacking with a Light melee weapon in one hand, spend Bonus Action for one attack with a different Light melee weapon in the other hand. Do not add a positive ability modifier to that attack's damage; include a negative modifier. This grants neither another Bonus Action nor a class feature.

## Movement and geometry

Track **spent movement cost**, not distance, for ordinary turn movement. Mode cap = `(1 + number of legal Dash actions this turn) × current Speed for that mode`; remaining = max(0, cap − spent cost). Switching modes never refunds cost. Recalculate cap on Speed changes, preserving spent cost. Stand from Prone costs floor(half current Speed) and requires nonzero Speed and enough budget. Dropping Prone costs no movement.

For each foot traveled, normal/difficult/severe terrain costs 1/2/3; use strongest terrain only. Add +1 for crawling and +1 for squeezing. Crawl in difficult =3:1; crawl+squeeze in difficult =4:1. A nonhostile occupied space counts as difficult terrain. Enter a hostile creature's space only with ≥two-size difference or an express feature; never willingly end in an occupied space. Squeeze through a space one size smaller, with own attack/DEX-save disadvantage and attacks-against advantage.

Walk10 through difficult spends20; switch to Fly30 leaves10. Stand with Speed30 spends15; switching to Fly30 leaves15. Two legal Dashes at Speed30 give cap90; prior expenditure still counts. Interpose/Press grant5 budget: they cannot enter a difficult 5-ft tile costing10. Their separate budgets never refill ordinary movement. Ready movement uses Speed at release; Withdraw uses half current Speed. All voluntary movement requires nonzero relevant Speed.

Forced movement does not use these ledgers or provoke ordinary opportunity attacks, cannot place creatures in solid matter, and stops at obstacles. Teleport does not traverse intervening terrain or spend Speed unless printed; it requires a legal destination. Speed0 alone does not block forced movement or teleport. Flight with Prone/Speed0/lost support causes Falling unless Hover or an explicit magical support exception applies; use the existing fixed Fall Marker procedure. Reaching support ends Falling.

NEW grid metric: each orthogonal or diagonal neighboring tile is 5 ft; use the same metric for ordinary ranges/areas. No diagonal cut through a corner when both adjacent orthogonal sides are completely blocked. Exact templates, 3D geometry, mounts, chases, jumping, climbing/swimming procedures and currents need further source review.

## Cover, sight, Hide and Search

Keep **visibility**, **known location**, and **line of effect** separate. Seeing through a barrier does not itself permit attacking through it. Best cover only: half +2 AC/DEX saves; three-quarters +5; total prevents direct targeting through that obstruction. Never add +2 and +5. For areas, evaluate line/cover from the area's origin to each target; total cover there excludes a creature unless the effect explicitly travels around/through the obstruction. The origin point itself must be legally targetable.

Within normal ranged range, no range penalty; beyond normal through long, disadvantage; beyond long, illegal. Ranged attacks also have disadvantage when within5ft of a non-Incapacitated hostile that can see the attacker, even if the attacker cannot see that hostile. All advantage/disadvantage follows the single cancellation rule.

NEW **Hide**: permitted only against observers unable to see you clearly or where a feature permits. Roll DEX Stealth once, record natural die and total. Against each observer's passive DC `10 + WIS Perception modifier including applicable proficiency`, use the Core four-degree task system. Meet DC succeeds before natural1/20 degree adjustment; Success/Critical Success establishes Hidden for that observer. No unsourced passive ±5 adjustment for advantage/disadvantage. Actual Search rolls use their normal modifiers.

Hidden means that observer cannot see you and does not know your location. Clear sight or learning your position ends Hidden for that observer. Moving between hiding places can retain Hidden only if no clear sight/revealing event occurs. A failed Hide cannot simply be rerolled unchanged; a materially new hiding position, concealment, distraction, sight-breaking event or express reroll feature is required. Hidden is not a generic immunity or a free stealth roll for every move.

**Search** Action: WIS Perception for sensory detection; INT Investigation for appropriate traces/deduction; Core Arcana/Engineering where relevant. Directly locating a Hidden creature defaults to Perception against its recorded final Stealth total as a static DC, using task degrees/natural shifts. Success reveals location only to the searcher and ends Hidden for that observer, without automatically granting sight. Communicate normally to inform others. Search can be repeated on later turns by paying the Action.

Attacks against unseen targets have disadvantage; unseen attackers have advantage, subject to the **Core Invisible exception**: once its position is known, Invisible's own attack modifiers end for that observer and cannot immediately return solely because it remains Invisible. Independent darkness, smoke, blindness or hiding can still supply modifiers.

Resolve an attack using pre-attack information; afterward reveal location to observers able to perceive its evidence. If an unseen target's location is unknown, do not directly choose it for a creature-target effect. A weapon/projectile can instead attack a suspected space: wrong space automatically misses; correct space uses unseen rules. A “creature you can see” requirement is never met by position knowledge alone. Area effects can target legal points under their own rules.

## Scope and validation

[Reference checks](COMBAT_PLAYTEST_VALIDATION.json) exercise selected budgets, clocks and edge cases. They do not implement map visibility, inventory, feature legality, complete event dispatch, all maneuvers or a live application. No claim of encounter balance or human playtest completion follows from a passing report.

`start_life_sequence` receives already-resolved/arbitrated HP events; it does not perform condition-family arbitration, cure processing, trigger generation, duration expiry, target legality, defense calculations or general simultaneous-event dispatch. `hide_results` answers only whether Hidden is established, collapsing the two success degrees and two failure degrees; it does not retain the full task-degree record. The Reaction helper models the ordinary resource, not additional Reaction grants printed on specific cards.

Remaining work includes cargo terminology/capacity/durability; detailed movement/environment/chases; area templates and sensory abilities; summons/boss full-turn examples; all-source class/spell semantic interactions and specific resource grants; and the whole-game ledger's other acceptance gates.


PENDING BLACKSMITHING GUIDE — DRAFT, NO COMPLETED EXACT VERDICT

# Blacksmithing recipes — campaign-playtest procedures

**Draft awaiting exact manuscript review.** The full 43-recipe source conference and eight-interface follow-up have settled the construction and effect model. No Blacksmithing card is yet counted as reviewed. Use this supplement with the [crafting framework](CRAFTING_HARVEST_PLAYTEST.md), [trade reference](CRAFTING_TRADE_REFERENCE.md), [equipment and acquisition rules](EQUIPMENT_ACQUISITION_PLAYTEST.md), [body/equipment rules](BODY_EQUIPMENT_PLAYTEST.md) and [combat procedure](COMBAT_ACTION_PLAYTEST.md).

Current complete source effects and numerical mechanics control legacy names, older effects, titles and optional guidance. All original fields remain preserved. A source `developed` status is not approval. The operational completions here are new provisional game rules, not quotations of recovered canon.

## Complete bodies and exact ingredients

Every finished output is a **durable magical nonartifact item** made by integrating the exact recipe into one actual complete mundane base item. It is not fabricated from an assumed mass of tiny magical ingredients. The base is a supplemental input; it replaces none of the source ingredients, tools, properties, tiers, formula access or work.

Before work, declare one legal base model, one fitted size and one actual equipment grade for the whole batch. Every reserved base uses that model/size/grade. Record each physical base identity and its actual maximum/current structural HP. A damaged but functioning base with at least 1 HP may qualify. A Broken base does not. An item already bearing a magical Blacksmithing recipe imprint is not a generic input for another recipe; replacement and upgrading require a separately reviewed procedure.

Reserve **floor(1.5 × source quantity)** complete legal bases before beginning the order. Each must be a real available object and cannot also serve another simultaneous order. Having money or knowing a supplier does not substitute for a reserved body. No source ingredient mass, returned catalyst, quality result or name creates additional base items.

| Source family | Legal base models |
|---|---|
| Sword | Shortsword, Longsword, Greatsword, Rapier, Scimitar |
| Hammer | Light hammer, Warhammer, Maul |
| Axe | Handaxe, Battleaxe, Greataxe |
| Mace | Mace, Flail, Morningstar |
| Spear | Spear, Javelin, Trident, Pike, Lance |
| Weapon | Dagger, Dart, Handaxe, Javelin, Light hammer, Mace, Sickle, Spear, Battleaxe, Flail, Glaive, Greataxe, Greatsword, Halberd, Lance, Longsword, Maul, Morningstar, Pike, Rapier, Scimitar, Shortsword, Trident, War pick, Warhammer, Kama |
| Shield | Buckler, Shield, Tower shield |
| Armor | Chain shirt, Scale mail, Breastplate, Half plate, Ring mail, Chain mail, Splint, Plate, Dwarven plate |
| Helm | The new Helm blank below |
| Gauntlets | The new matched Gauntlets pair below |

A specific card can bind one model from this table. In particular, Alien-Chitin Lamellar uses **Scale mail**: AC 14 + min(DEX modifier, 2), Stealth disadvantage, 45 lb and 50 gp at the ordinary Standard Small/Medium baseline. Its name does not create another armor formula. The internal cards retain any separately gated specific bindings.

Generic Weapon excludes bows, crossbows, repeating crossbows, firearms, ammunition, blowguns, nets, natural weapons, clubs, greatclubs, quarterstaffs, slings, whips and nunchaku. Dart is an actual discrete thrown weapon, not an ammunition batch. Legal model choice grants no new proficiency or permission to use the item.

## Base custody, transformation and mass

The exact transformed base ceases to exist as a separate mundane inventory asset. Preserve its identity/history within the finished item. Standard transforms source quantity Q; Exceptional yield transforms floor(1.25Q); Masterwork yield transforms floor(1.5Q); Flawed Q≥2 transforms floor(Q/2); singleton Flawed transforms one body. A structural-improvement result transforms Standard quantity. Every unused reserved base remains an actual mundane object with its actual condition.

Failed and Mishap produce no recipe imprint. Reserved bases return as mundane items unless an independently resolved physical event actually damages one. Source ingredients still undergo the framework's exact recovery/loss. The supplemental base is not a printed recipe component and receives no extra 25% recovery credit. A recovery entry cannot duplicate the still-existing body.

Source tools, including nonconsumed Forge water, contribute zero finished structural mass. Fuel and solvent also contribute zero. Binder, substrate, material and catalyst occurrences remain required, but do not add their listed mass to the finished operational weight. The finished weight is the selected base's actual weight, including legitimate size/grade effects. Process mass becomes slag, trimmings or other waste without an automatic salvage or market identity. A legally retained Masterwork catalyst returns with its original identity and remaining age; it is not also incorporated into the finished body.

**Example — five axes:** recipe-45 has Q5 and maximum yield 7. Seven Medium Standard Battleaxes require seven actual 4-lb, 10-gp bases. Standard transforms five and returns two mundane axes. Masterwork yield transforms seven. The source four Brightsteel Ingots and putrid marrow are paid once as printed; their combined 4.18 lb does not supply seven axe bodies.

**Example — five shields:** recipe-53 has Q5 and maximum yield 7. Choosing Tower shield requires seven actual 15-lb, 50-gp Standard bases. Standard transforms five and returns two. The source's 5.43 lb of recipe inputs does not replace the 75 lb of transformed shield bodies.

**Ordered reserves:** before any active work, record every maximum-yield base in a stable ordered list, including model, size, grade, dominant load-bearing material, current/maximum HP and persistent durability modifiers. Transform the first N entries for the resolved quantity, or the first Standard Q for the durability option. Return all other actual bodies unchanged. No reordering, replacement, acquisition or selection of the best bodies after seeing the roll. If a base is missing, committed elsewhere or Broken before the completion check, pause the order and repair or replace it before rolling. A replacement occupies that exact list position with its own recorded condition. Damaged but non-Broken bases remain legal.

## Weapon benefits

An intact, unsuppressed weapon adds its printed recipe attack bonus only to an attack actually made with that physical weapon. Legal melee, off-hand and thrown attacks qualify. Carrying it gives no general attack bonus. Ordinary proficiency, ability, damage dice, reach, range, properties and action costs remain those of the selected model and the actual attack procedure.

The recipe damage bonus applies **once** to the attack's ordinary weapon-damage component on a hit. It still applies when the actual off-hand rule omits ordinary ability damage. It is not multiplied across damage types. If a reviewed effect changes that component's damage type, the flat bonus follows the actual resulting component. Flat bonuses never double on a critical. Legal attacks on objects qualify only where the weapon and actual targeting/damage rules permit them.

An unsuppressed Blacksmith weapon's ordinary bludgeoning, piercing or slashing damage counts as magical for defenses that distinguish magical damage. This does not change its damage type or confer spell rank, spellcasting, Counterspell eligibility, RP, concentration, attunement or an activation Action. Source elemental tags alone grant no elemental damage.

**New item-accuracy family:** the preserved V6 weapon-grade attack modifier and the recipe's attack bonus use the stronger value rather than adding. A Fine +1 grade weapon with recipe +2 uses +2 item accuracy. An Exceptional +3 grade weapon with recipe +1 uses +3 item accuracy and the recipe's +1 damage. Superior/Exceptional grade critical dice remain separate because they are not flat accuracy modifiers.

Other class, spell or item bonuses follow their actual reviewed combination rules. This family is not a universal strongest-bonus rule. No generic second recipe imprint can stack onto the body. Source names such as Superior, Masterwork or Legendary do not grant an equipment grade. The actual supplied mundane base retains its legal grade; a new mundane base without a different established grade is Standard.

Evaluate only real applicable modifiers. Crude weapon grade −1 and active recipe +1 use +1; suppressing the recipe leaves the actual −1. There is no phantom Standard +0 that cancels a remaining penalty. Broken items supply no normal weapon function to enhance. A separately legal improvised use does not recover the item's ordinary weapon or Blacksmith bonuses.

## Printed elemental riders

Only a card whose current `bonusDamage` actually contains dice receives this procedure. Its wielder may apply those exact dice/type when hitting a legal target with an attack actually made using the intact, unsuppressed physical weapon. Choose after the hit is known and before finalizing the attack's damage. Declining does not spend the budget.

The additional typed component belongs to the **same attack damage event**. It is not another attack, hit, action, target, save or proc. Its attack damage dice double normally on a critical; the flat recipe damage bonus does not. Apply actual defenses to each type within the same event and the ordinary rules for that event's consequences. Do not manufacture extra damage events to multiply Wounds or other thresholds.

The frequency identity is **physical item + actual turn instance**, with one use for that item during that turn. Two physical weapons each have their own budget, including different recipes or two copies. Transfer during the turn does not refresh an item's used budget. Extra Actions during the same turn do not refresh it. An actual extra turn is a new turn instance. A legal Reaction, opportunity attack or readied attack on another creature's turn uses the item's budget for that actual turn.

A legal attack on an ally may qualify, with the rider still optional; this grants no generic self-attack permission. A legal object hit may receive the rider, subject to actual object defenses and physical compatibility. Psychic immunity still prevents a psychic damage component.

Copied attacks or damage create no new rider trigger, refresh or second expenditure. A copy of an already-resolved event follows its own reviewed rule for the recorded damage. A companion gains no weapon permission or proficiency from the item. If an independently reviewed companion rule permits an attack using that actual weapon, its attack may use the item's remaining budget for the turn under the same procedure.

**Outside initiative:** a genuinely out-of-initiative lawful attack uses one budget per absolute six-second combat-action window. The first relevant combat-scale attack establishes the item's current window; additional lawful attacks within it do not create more budgets. A new window requires six complete elapsed seconds. This grants no attacks or acceleration. If the attempted attack begins an encounter, first establish initiative and resolve its actual legal entry/turn procedure; it receives no earlier outside budget.

Track `lastElementalRiderTriggerTime` across mode changes. If the item triggered outside initiative and initiative begins before six seconds have elapsed, its first actual turn before that deadline treats the rider as already spent. After that transition guard is satisfied, actual regular/extra-turn budgets apply normally. Ending initiative after an initiative trigger prevents another outside trigger until six seconds after that last trigger; normal outside windows then apply. Merely toggling bookkeeping never refreshes the item.

## Armor, shields and defensive enhancement

The printed recipe AC bonus is an **enhancement**, not a replacement for the entire base armor formula or shield bonus. Keep the selected suit's normal formula, full or capped DEX modifier, Strength requirement, Stealth rule, proficiency, fitting, weight and don/doff. Keep a shield's actual hands, Strength, proficiency, weight and don/doff requirements.

**New `equipment.defense-enhancement` family:** all active Blacksmith recipe AC bonuses and the applicable V6 worn-armor grade AC modifier use the strongest single value. They do not add together. The base shield's normal +1/+2/+3 is separate from this enhancement family.

- A suit's recipe enhancement qualifies when that suit supplies the worn-armor AC formula actually being used.
- A shield's recipe enhancement qualifies when it is the one usable shield contributing its ordinary shield bonus.
- One worn, intact and unsuppressed Helm and one Gauntlets pair can each supply a candidate enhancement.
- The actual worn suit's grade modifier qualifies under its normal armor rules.

Independent class, spell, ancestry and natural/unarmored rules retain their own wording. A suit's unused formula cannot contribute its recipe bonus as though it were an accessory. The new accessories do not themselves count as armor suits.

An ordinary Shield with recipe-53 contributes its base +2 and, if strongest, its recipe +1, for a total +3. If a higher eligible defensive enhancement is already present, the shield still supplies its base +2, while its lower enhancement adds nothing. Actual Tower shield requirements remain in force when that model is chosen; crafting does not waive them.

Resistance is a distinct printed benefit. An item's lower AC enhancement can be inactive while its printed resistance remains active under its own wearing requirements. Different resistance types coexist. Multiple resistance sources to the same type do not become immunity. No title, ingredient or metadata tag grants resistance absent the current effect.

Each lawfully donned, intact and unsuppressed shield supplies its printed resistance even if another shield supplies ordinary shield AC. Only the selected AC-contributing shield offers its recipe enhancement. Every donned shield still occupies its actual hand/forearm and retains its Strength, Stealth, proficiency and other drawbacks. A back-slung, carried, stored or dropped shield is not donned and supplies neither benefit. Same-type resistance still does not become immunity.

Shield bases default to Standard. An actual preexisting shield with an independently reviewed grade retains only the properties and valuation that its actual prior rule established. Suit-only grade AC, weight or HP rules are not imported onto shields. Blacksmithing creates no shield-grade entitlement.

The family considers actual modifiers only: a Crude suit's −1 and active recipe +1 contribute +1; suppressing the recipe leaves −1. No implicit +0 cancels it. At Broken, the item's normal positive armor/shield function ceases; the physical drawbacks remain rather than evaluating enhancement arithmetic for a function the item no longer supplies.

## New Helm and Gauntlets bodies

| Profile | Helm | Gauntlets |
|---|---|---|
| One output unit | One helm | One matched pair, not one glove |
| Ordinary Standard baseline price | 10 gp | 10 gp per pair |
| Ordinary weight | 5 lb | 4 lb per pair |
| Dominant material / structural AC | Iron/steel / 19 | Iron/steel / 19 |
| Ordinary maximum structural HP | 5 | 5 for the whole pair as one tracked object |
| Slot | One head slot | One gauntlet/handwear pair slot |
| Don / doff | One Action each | One Action for the pair, each way |

Only one benefit-bearing helm and one benefit-bearing pair can be worn in those respective slots. Additional copies are carried/stowed objects. These bodies have Standard equipment grade only; no separate Fine/Superior/Exceptional accessory-grade progression has been authored.

These are defensive accessories, not full light/medium/heavy armor. They require no armor proficiency and create no generic Strength minimum or Stealth penalty. Wearing them alone does not violate a rule requiring no armor suit. A suit descriptively including gloves or a helmet does not create extra accessory slots or prevent these separate accessories.

Gauntlets occupy the handwear slot but do not occupy the hands themselves. The wearer may hold weapons, manipulate objects, perform somatic components and manipulate a legal focus under the ordinary hand rules. They supply no additional free hand and are not new weapons or unarmed-strike bonuses.

For Large fittings, price, weight and structural HP each double, with no increase in AC, damage or other properties. Small/Medium/Tiny keep the listed operational profile unless an actual rule says otherwise. Ordinary body/equipment rules govern fit, melding, return and actual carrying weight.

## Structural state and suppression

Every item separately records its base model, size, actual equipment grade, ordinary and actual maximum/current HP, source recipe imprint, crafting-quality modifier and source/base/finished values. These fields are not one generic quality tier. Structural AC and HP need their explicitly reviewed base bindings; magical durability is not inferred for unrelated magic items.

While magic is legally suppressed, keep the mundane base's ordinary usability, size, grade, mass, hand/slot use, armor formula, Strength minimum, Stealth, don/doff and physical drawbacks. Recipe accuracy, flat damage, elemental rider, AC enhancement, resistance and recipe-derived magical B/P/S classification are inactive. Suppression supplies no repair, new attack, catalyst, proc or budget refresh.

If a rider was already used in the current turn, suppressing and unsuppressing the item leaves it used. If unused, it may become available when legal suppression ends within that same turn. Suppression does not reveal any origin or cosmological explanation.

At 0 structural HP the whole item is **Broken**. Its mundane positive function and recipe benefits stop. The actual body, weight, occupied hand/slot, wearing drawbacks and don/doff remain. The imprint is dormant while a recoverable main body exists; it is not automatically erased. Ordinary rest restores neither HP nor function. A missing/destroyed main body cannot be recovered merely by naming its former imprint.

**Explicit structural binding for this family:** ordinary weapons HP5, Buckler/Shield HP10, Tower shield HP15, Medium armor HP15, Heavy armor HP20, Helm HP5 and Gauntlets pair HP5. Large doubles the applicable baseline before durability modifiers. The actual base's predeclared dominant load-bearing material supplies its reviewed structural AC: iron/steel 19, wood/leather 15, or another genuinely applicable reviewed material profile. Mixed bodies use the material actually carrying most structural load, recorded before work, never selected after an attack. An ingredient tag does not change it. Worn/held Sunder still uses the higher of that material AC and the wearer's actual AC under the equipment procedure.

Crafting durability increases and Flawed maximum reductions are physical workmanship records. Suppression changes no maximum/current HP, damage deficit, flaw, durability increase, material AC, size or mundane grade. Ending suppression restores only otherwise legal magical functions, without healing, removing a flaw or refreshing a budget.

## Craft quality and preexisting damage

Resolve the crafting framework's six outcomes, natural-1 Mishap and numeric margins. Natural 20 grants no extra band shift. Forge Dwarf's actual artisan-creation permission reduces time and treats an actual Standard result as Exceptional output; it does not upgrade Flawed, Failed, Mishap or an already higher result, grant equipment grade or add XP.

Choose quality yield or the structural option once for the whole batch after the check. The structural option keeps Standard quantity and raises an explicitly known ordinary maximum by 25% for Exceptional or 50% for Masterwork, rounded down, minimum +1. The eligible Masterwork catalyst return remains one actual consumed catalyst unit total, independent of yield versus durability. Repeated same-name/ID component occurrences still require their separate actual quantities.

Mundane nonmagical, non-Broken bases may already have a reviewed crafting durability increase or persistent singleton-Flawed maximum. Use one noncompounding calculation:

1. B is ordinary maximum HP after the actual model, size and equipment-grade HP rule, including Crude suit HP where applicable.
2. P is the strongest legally attached positive crafting durability percentage: 0, 25% or 50%. A new structural option replaces P only if stronger; it never multiplies an already improved maximum.
3. ImprovedMax = B + floor(B × P), with a minimum +1 increase when P>0.
4. If a persistent singleton-Flawed state exists, FinalMax = max(1, floor(ImprovedMax / 2)); otherwise FinalMax = ImprovedMax. A new singleton Flawed result establishes that one state, never a second halving.
5. Preserve DamageDeficit = OldMax − OldCurrent. NewCurrent = max(0, FinalMax − DamageDeficit), capped at FinalMax.

Standard preserves the existing modifiers and damage. A 3/5 weapon gaining +1 maximum becomes 4/6, not 6/6. A B20 base with +25% durability and a persistent flaw has max12. Replacing that increase with +50% produces max15; it still has the flaw and preserves its damage deficit. A badly damaged base may emerge Broken after a new Flawed result.

For Q≥2, Flawed changes output quantity to floor(Q/2), not unit potency or existing durability modifiers. For Q1 it establishes the one persistent flaw as above. Neither later durability improvement, suppression nor ordinary repair erases that flaw. No generic flaw-removal, recipe replacement or repeated-imprint upgrade is granted.

## Repair and reconstruction

For a functioning Blacksmith output with HP≥1, the imprint is intact. The reviewed structural repair uses actual Smith's tools, Forge and Anvil, and Tongs; choose STR or INT before the check and apply actual Smith's-tools proficiency once. DC10; active work max(1 hour, actual maximum structural HP / 10 hours); materials cost 25% of its mundane size/grade base reference. The recipe's magic/process value is not part of this functioning-item material price. Success restores current HP to the recorded maximum; failure spends the actual work and materials.

For a Broken output at HP0 with its recoverable main body, use the new reconstruction interface. The same actual tools/station and STR-or-INT choice apply. DC is the original recipe crafting DC. Active work is max(8 hours, actual maximum structural HP / 10 hours). Materials cost 25% of the undamaged **finished per-item reference**, including its source magic/process share and actual mundane base reference. Round an actual purchase under the ordinary currency procedure.

Reconstruction is a **binary repair task**, not the six-band crafting check. Success restores current HP to its existing maximum and restores the dormant imprint's usability, subject to any continuing suppression. Failure spends materials/time and leaves the item Broken. There is no yield, catalyst, quality roll, crafting XP or first-success award. Forge Dwarf's creation-time reduction does not shorten reconstruction.

Repair preserves model, size, grade, recipe identity, existing quality, any Flawed maximum and any recorded noncompounding HP increase. It cannot replace a missing main body, generate recipe ingredients, remove a flaw, stack a second imprint or upgrade the recipe. A genuinely missing body needs a new craft and new actual inputs. An available Core repair service retains its separately quoted source procedure and does not receive a second automatic self-repair charge.

Both repair procedures require actual **Smith's-tools proficiency**. Owning a kit or holding a trade level does not supply it. Neither procedure requires formula ownership or the original crafting recipe's trade-level eligibility: it restores the surviving imprinted object rather than creating another. Repairing a restricted item does not teach or grant its formula. Forge Dwarf's creation-only time reduction applies to neither functioning-item repair nor HP0 reconstruction. The generic binary repair outcome gives no crafting quality, XP, catalyst or extra result.

## Work phases, stations and source access

Use STR or INT with actual Smith's-tools proficiency where applicable, the exact recipe crafting DC and the framework's lawful tool/facility/assistant modifiers. Owning tools does not grant proficiency. Blacksmithing is an artisan creation trade. Keep the actual source work hours, formula and trade-level requirements, assistant cap and work/rest limits.

Compute lawful creation-time adjustments first. The first stable integration phase is **min(8 active hours, final actual required work time)**. It is included in total work. For an eligible Forge Dwarf, source 8 hours becomes 6 actual hours and a six-hour first phase; source 4 becomes 3 and a three-hour phase; source 80 becomes 60 with an eight-hour first phase. Later phases use the ordinary framework. Assistant hours follow the same actual order duration and do not independently shorten it.

At the first stable phase's completion, perishable/reactive recipe components already incorporated into that order become stable within its workpieces. Before then, their real preservation clocks continue. Unincorporated reserved components keep their own clocks. This is neither extractable preserved raw stock nor an early completion roll or second ingredient charge. Actual phase interruption rules still apply.

The station `Forge and Anvil` and the required Forge/Anvil tools can be satisfied by the same actual operational fixtures. They do not require two forges and two anvils. Actual Smith's tools and Tongs must also be available. Ordinary unquantified heat/fuel is part of operating that station; invent no extra quantified coal bill. A printed Coal component remains its separate required consumed input. Printed Forge water retains its exact component identity and consumed flag; it is not free generic water or structural mass.

All 43 source recipes have **fieldCraftable:false**. That flag controls even if a preserved source `timeMode` says field. The label does not permit portable field crafting or conjure a Forge. Expedition use requires a real eligible worksite supplying the actual Forge and Anvil. Preserve contradictory source labels rather than silently rewriting them. Optional Director guidance remains optional and adds no automatic ritual, extra ingredient or extra hours.

## Reference value and access

Preserve the source recommendedMarketValue as the **Standard source batch reference**, not a per-item price. For source quantity Q:

`source magic/process unit reference = source batch reference / Q`

`finished per-item reference = source magic/process unit reference + actual undamaged mundane size/grade base reference`

Keep exact rational values until the actual transaction rounding step. An output count N gives N finished items with their actual per-item references. Crafting quality alone gives no market multiplier. Buying supplemental bases is a separate real expense; owned bases need no repurchase. Neither changes original consumedMaterialValue, laborValue or source economy fields.

Recipe-42 has source batch value 92 gp and Q1. A Standard Medium Longsword base adds 15 gp, giving a 107-gp finished reference. Large sizing doubles the base to 30 gp, giving 122 gp; it does not double the 92-gp source field. Recipe-53 has 94 gp/Q5 = 18.8 gp source share. A Standard ordinary Shield adds 10 gp, giving 28.8 gp per finished unit before an actual quote.

Finished outputs are magical nonartifact equipment. Use a legitimate finite buyer and actual quote; the mundane standing resale table is not an automatic guaranteed magic-item buyback. Damage, flaws, scarcity, law, reputation and demand retain their actual quote consequences, with no newly invented universal adjustment. Magical status or high tier alone does not invoke artifact valuation. HP0 reconstruction uses the same undamaged per-item finished reference.

Recipe knowledge and public/Director visibility remain exactly as sourced. A identified item or repaired body does not silently grant a crafting formula. Material/monster names provide neither availability rights nor approved creature statistics. `wholeCreatureUse` metadata grants no donor powers or anatomy traits. Only current `mechanics.tag` is operative; secondary source tags and legacy effects remain preserved but supply no additional damage, resistance, healing, poison classification or regeneration.

## Scope and remaining exact review

This supplement implements the source conference and all eight focused interface decisions. The complete cards and machine projections still need exact review and source-fidelity checks. Human numerical balance, ingredient access, wider unfinished class/spell interactions, runtime/companion parity and whole-game flow remain separate unfinished gates. No source information is deleted. No GitHub push has occurred. Cosmology remains deferred until the authorized push succeeds and the remote commit is verified.


UNRESOLVED CONCRETE REVIEW SCENARIOS

# Leatherworking decision scenarios — unresolved

These cases are questions for the source conference. They do not implement a proposed rule or assert a passing gameplay result. The linked source packet includes the relevant recipes and existing guides.

| Case | Concrete setup | Required decision |
|---|---|---|
| LW-01 | Public recipe-308 has armorBonus=1; its exact V5 effect mentions only +2 to a related check. | Choose and record explicit source precedence; retain both originals. |
| LW-02 | A wearer combines recipe-319 armorBonus=2, recipe-653 boots armorBonus=1, an existing Fine suit and a functioning Blacksmith accessory. | Resolve one legal armor formula and exact bonus families, after Blacksmithing's actual final review. |
| LW-03 | Recipe-295 promises 20 lb relief on a Pouch; an ordinary pouch holds 6 lb/0.2 cubic feet. | Define the actual finished body's physical capacity and usable relief without silently shrinking the promise. |
| LW-04 | A recipe-280 backpack contains 20 lb of ordinary contents. Empty mundane backpack weight is 5 lb; the source relief number is 10 lb. | Specify actual pounds, inventory Load, burden and support weight separately. |
| LW-05 | A recipe-290 pouch sits inside a recipe-297 backpack, with the same 8 lb of contents nested inside both. | Assign each actual item one mass record and prevent repeated relief on the same content; account for both empty bodies. |
| LW-06 | A recipe-297 backpack encloses an ordinary sack containing two full waterskins and a separately measured rock. | Apply inclusive 5-lb waterskin weights once, not plus duplicate water; decide which nested contents are eligible. |
| LW-07 | A character wears two magical backpacks, a belt and two pouches. One backpack is strapped outside the other. | Define legal benefit slots and actual custody; distinguish contents from externally attached gear. |
| LW-08 | A container holds an item with printed Load=3 and documented weight=1 lb. Another holds 50 coins. | Resolve whether relief acts on pounds, Load or both; do not automatically erase printed Load by converting it to weight. |
| LW-09 | A character hands a partially filled functioning container to an ally mid-turn. | Transfer actual contents once, apply the correct recipient/position requirements, and preserve capacity and condition. |
| LW-10 | Suppression or structural breakage turns off the relief on a container whose bearer is already at carrying capacity. | Recalculate overload and legal containment/placement without deleting, duplicating or teleporting inventory. |
| LW-11 | A voluntary Druid form change begins with containers that only fit the original burden allowance while their magic functions. | Determine preflight and fixed meld budgets under the current body rules; worn/held functions suspend in meld. |
| LW-12 | An ally carries that transformed character with the containers and all their contents melded. | Preserve body weight, casualty Load and full equipment records; identify any explicitly lawful relief once. |
| LW-13 | Gloves and boots could both be described as helping one climbing check; a tool and class feature also apply. | Define exact qualifying tasks and stacking without creating proficiency, extra Speed or multiple bonuses from vague descriptions. |
| LW-14 | A character retrieves an easy-to-reach item, then tries a second retrieval, then an ally retrieves from the same pack. | Preserve interaction/Action costs and hand/access needs; identify when a check exists and whose bonus applies. |
| LW-15 | A thief attempts to open someone else's container against its wearer. | Determine whether secure/retrieve bonuses apply to either actor's actual check and avoid automatic theft prevention. |
| LW-16 | Recipe-280 Q=4 consumes 1.44 lb of components; Masterwork yield could be six 5-lb backpack bodies. | Provide complete predeclared material/body supply and finite economic accounting; source components stay required. |
| LW-17 | A body reserved for an order is damaged, broken or unavailable before the completion check; the result is Flawed or Failed. | Specify reserve eligibility and return/consumption rules before any post-roll selection advantage. |
| LW-18 | A one-unit prior-flawed body gains a new structural quality improvement and is later repaired at zero HP. | Define noncompounding flaw/improvement layers, preservation of damage, recoverable-body requirement and actual repair credential. |
| LW-19 | Recipe-319 has null source active hours and one-year source time. A Forge Dwarf crafts it across interrupted Watches. | Retain the source null; label the 1,920-hour framework overlay, apply only lawful time reduction, and define incorporation/phases. |
| LW-20 | Recipe-653 boots enter acid; recipe-655 coat enters a freezing environment. | Boots have no source acid resistance. Coat's cold-damage resistance does not itself define environmental immunity. Armor-field conflicts remain separately recorded. |

Every resulting card must also preserve its original formula identity, visibility, required skill, tools/station, every consumed component occurrence, source quantity/value and lawful access. All 201 component occurrences are consumed and none is a catalyst, so there is no eligible source catalyst for Masterwork to return.
